- **Extracted knowledge**:
  Concept: Set definition as a well-defined collection of distinct objects.
  Definitions:
  - Set: A collection of well-defined, distinct objects or elements.
  - Well-defined: A property of a collection that makes it possible to objectively determine whether any given object belongs to the collection or not.
  Examples:
  - Prime factors of 36 (well-defined) vs. beautiful girls/handsome boys in a class (subjective).
  Activities:
  - Activity 1'.1: Categorizing lists of collections into sets or subjective groups.
  Connections: Baseline for algebraic relations and functions [CONNECTION].

### Page 5 -> Unit 1': Further on Sets
- **Source**: Grade 9 Maths Student Textbook 2Aug22.pdf
- **Textbook**: Grade 9 Mathematics Student Textbook
- **Unit/Section**: Unit 1': Further on Sets
- **Page**: 5
- **Status**: EXTRACTED
- **Extracted knowledge**:
  Concept: One-to-one correspondence, subset inclusion, and proper subset exclusion.
  Definitions:
  - Equivalent Sets (A <-> B or A ~ B): Two sets are equivalent if and only if there is a one-to-one correspondence between their elements.
  - Subset (A <= B): Every element of A is also an element of B.
  - Proper Subset (A < B): A is a subset of B and A != B.
  Rules/Theorems:
  - A finite set with n elements has 2^n subsets and 2^n - 1 proper subsets.
  Connections: Permutations and combinatorics basis [CONNECTION].

### Page 9 -> Unit 1': Further on Sets
- **Source**: Grade 9 Maths Student Textbook 2Aug22.pdf
- **Textbook**: Grade 9 Mathematics Student Textbook
- **Unit/Section**: Unit 1': Further on Sets
- **Page**: 9
- **Status**: EXTRACTED
- **Extracted knowledge**:
  Concept: Set operations (Union, Intersection, Relative Complement).
  Definitions:
  - Union (A U B): The set of all elements in A, in B, or in both.
  - Intersection (A n B): The set of all elements simultaneously in both A and B.
  - Relative Complement (A - B or A \ B): The set of all elements in A that are not in B.
  Formulas:
  - A - B = A n B'
  Questions:
  - Exercise 1'.6: Find A', B', (A U B)', and (A' n B') for factor sets under U = {1..20}.
  Connections: Boolean operations (OR, AND, NOT) mapping [CONNECTION].

### Page 11 -> Unit 1': Further on Sets
- **Source**: Grade 9 Maths Student Textbook 2Aug22.pdf
- **Textbook**: Grade 9 Mathematics Student Textbook
- **Unit/Section**: Unit 1': Further on Sets
- **Page**: 11
- **Status**: EXTRACTED
- **Extracted knowledge**:
  Concept: Set algebra, identity laws, and dual representations.
  Definitions:
  - Universal Set (U): Overarching set under consideration.
  - Empty Set (Ø): Unique set containing no elements.
  Rules/Theorems:
  - Double Complement Law: (A')' = A
  - De Morgan's Laws: (1) (A U B)' = A' n B', (2) (A n B)' = A' U B'
  - Inclusion-Reversal: A <= B <=> B' <= A'
  - Laws of Ø and U: A n U = A, A n Ø = Ø
  Examples:
  - Numerical verification of De Morgan's laws using divisor sets.

### Page 12 -> Unit 1': Further on Sets
- **Source**: Grade 9 Maths Student Textbook 2Aug22.pdf
- **Textbook**: Grade 9 Mathematics Student Textbook
- **Unit/Section**: Unit 1': Further on Sets
- **Page**: 12
- **Status**: EXTRACTED
- **Extracted knowledge**:
  Concept: Symmetric difference.
  Definitions:
  - Symmetric Difference (A Δ B): Set containing elements in A or in B, but not in both.
  Formulas:
  - A Δ B = (A - B) U (B - A) = (A U B) - (A n B)
  Worked Examples:
  - Given A = {1,2,4,5,8}, B = {2,3,5,7}. Intersection is {2,5}. Union is {1,2,3,4,5,7,8}. Difference A Δ B = {1,3,4,7,8}.
  Connections: Equivalent to XOR boolean logic gates in computer science [CONNECTION].

### Page 13 -> Unit 1': Further on Sets
- **Source**: Grade 9 Maths Student Textbook 2Aug22.pdf
- **Textbook**: Grade 9 Mathematics Student Textbook
- **Unit/Section**: Unit 1': Further on Sets
- **Page**: 13
- **Status**: EXTRACTED
- **Extracted knowledge**:
  Concept: Coordinate mapping and Cartesian products.
  Definitions:
  - Cartesian Product (A x B): The set of all ordered pairs (a, b) where a in A and b in B.
  Rules:
  - Cardinality: n(A x B) = n(A) * n(B)
  - Non-commutativity: A x B != B x A (unless A = B or one is empty).
  Worked Examples:
  - A = {2,4,6}, B = {1,3} -> A x B = {(2,1), (2,3), (4,1), (4,3), (6,1), (6,3)}.
  Connections: Basis of coordinate geometry, functions, and mapping in G10 [CONNECTION].

### Page 14 -> Unit 1': Further on Sets
- **Source**: Grade 9 Maths Student Textbook 2Aug22.pdf
- **Textbook**: Grade 9 Mathematics Student Textbook
- **Unit/Section**: Unit 1': Further on Sets
- **Page**: 14
- **Status**: EXTRACTED
- **Extracted knowledge**:
  Concept: Set applications and surveyed counts.
  Formulas:
  - Cardinality of Union: n(A U B) = n(A) + n(B) - n(A n B)
  Worked Examples:
  - Given n(A) = 20, n(B) = 28, n(A U B) = 36. Rearranging formula: n(A n B) = 20 + 28 - 36 = 12.
  Connections: Corresponds to probability addition rule P(A U B) = P(A) + P(B) - P(A n B) in G9 Unit 8 [CONNECTION].

- **Extracted knowledge**:
  Concept: Euclid's Division Lemma.
  Rules/Theorems:
  - Euclid's Division Lemma: Given integers a >= 0 and b > 0, there exist unique non-negative integers q and r such that a = (b * q) + r, where 0 <= r < b.
  Worked Examples:
  - 128 divided by 6 yields q = 21, r = 2.
  Connections: Fundamental in modular arithmetic, computing indexing, and computer science [CONNECTION].

### Page 21 -> Unit 1: The Number System
- **Source**: Grade 9 Maths Student Textbook 2Aug22.pdf
- **Textbook**: Grade 9 Mathematics Student Textbook
- **Unit/Section**: Unit 1: The Number System
- **Page**: 21
- **Status**: EXTRACTED
- **Extracted knowledge**:
  Concept: Prime and composite numbers.
  Definitions:
  - Prime Number: A natural number greater than 1 with exactly two factors: 1 and itself.
  - Composite Number: A natural number with more than two factors.
  - Prime Factorization: Expressing a composite number as a product of prime bases.
  Rules/Theorems:
  - The Fundamental Theorem of Arithmetic: Every composite number can be uniquely expressed as a product of prime numbers, disregarding their order.
  Worked Examples:
  - Prime factorization of 120 = 2^3 * 3 * 5.

### Page 23 -> Unit 1: The Number System
- **Source**: Grade 9 Maths Student Textbook 2Aug22.pdf
- **Textbook**: Grade 9 Mathematics Student Textbook
- **Unit/Section**: Unit 1: The Number System
- **Page**: 23
- **Status**: EXTRACTED
- **Extracted knowledge**:
  Concept: Divisors and multiples.
  Definitions:
  - GCF: Greatest Common Factor of multiple integers.
  - LCM: Least Common Multiple of multiple integers.
  Rules/Theorems:
  - GCF-LCM Theorem: GCF(a, b) * LCM(a, b) = a * b.
  Worked Examples:
  - GCF and LCM of 24 and 60. Factorization: 24 = 2^3 * 3, 60 = 2^2 * 3 * 5.
  - GCF = 2^2 * 3 = 12. LCM = 2^3 * 3 * 5 = 120.
  - Verification: 12 * 120 = 1440; 24 * 60 = 1440.
  Questions:
  - Exercise 1.9: Verification of division remainders.

  Concept: Terminating and repeating decimals.
  Worked Examples:
  - Convert repeating decimal 0.555... to a fraction.
    Let x = 0.555... -> 10x = 5.555... -> 9x = 5 -> x = 5/9.
  - Convert 2.1212... to a fraction.
    Let x = 2.1212... -> 100x = 212.1212... -> 99x = 210 -> x = 210/99 = 70/33.

### Page 58 -> Unit 1: The Number System
- **Source**: Grade 9 Maths Student Textbook 2Aug22.pdf
- **Textbook**: Grade 9 Mathematics Student Textbook
- **Unit/Section**: Unit 1: The Number System
- **Page**: 58
- **Status**: EXTRACTED
- **Extracted knowledge**:
  Concept: Exponent rules and radical simplifications.
  Rules:
  - sqrt(a) * sqrt(b) = sqrt(ab) for non-negative numbers.
  - sqrt(a/b) = sqrt(a) / sqrt(b).
  - Rationalizing denominators by multiplying by conjugates.
  Worked Examples:
  - Express 3*sqrt(2) as sqrt(18).
  - Simplify -sqrt(18) / sqrt(2) = -3.
  - Rationalize 1 / (2 - sqrt(6)) by multiplying by (2 + sqrt(6)) yielding -(2 + sqrt(6))/2.

### Page 74 -> Unit 1: The Number System
- **Source**: Grade 9 Maths Student Textbook 2Aug22.pdf
- **Textbook**: Grade 9 Mathematics Student Textbook
- **Unit/Section**: Unit 1: The Number System
- **Page**: 74
- **Status**: EXTRACTED
- **Extracted knowledge**:
  Concept: Measurement uncertainty and calculations.
  Rules:
  - Upper bound of a sum/product is the sum/product of the upper bounds.
  - Lower bound of a sum/product is the sum/product of the lower bounds.
  Worked Examples:
  - Rectangular land of length 12m, width 6m (to nearest meter).
    Length range: [11.5, 12.5), Width range: [5.5, 6.5).
    Lower bound of Area = 11.5 * 5.5 = 63.25 m^2.
    Upper bound of Area = 12.5 * 6.5 = 81.25 m^2.
  Connections: Direct link to scientific measurement uncertainty in G9 Physics Unit 2 and Chemistry Unit 2 [CONNECTION].

### Page 92 -> Unit 2: Solving Equations
- **Source**: Grade 9 Maths Student Textbook 2Aug22.pdf
- **Textbook**: Grade 9 Mathematics Student Textbook
- **Unit/Section**: Unit 2: Solving Equations
- **Page**: 92
- **Status**: EXTRACTED
- **Extracted knowledge**:
  Concept: Solving systems using substitution.
  Rules:
  1. Express one variable in terms of the other from one equation.
  2. Substitute into the second equation and solve.
  3. Substitute back to find the first variable.
  Worked Examples:
  - Solve 2x + y = 15, 3x - y = 5 -> y = 15 - 2x -> 3x - (15 - 2x) = 5 -> 5x = 20 -> x = 4, y = 7. Solution (4, 7).

### Page 93 -> Unit 2: Solving Equations
- **Source**: Grade 9 Maths Student Textbook 2Aug22.pdf
- **Textbook**: Grade 9 Mathematics Student Textbook
- **Unit/Section**: Unit 2: Solving Equations
- **Page**: 93
- **Status**: EXTRACTED
- **Extracted knowledge**:
  Concept: Solving systems using elimination.
  Rules:
  - Multiply equations by scalars to match coefficients of one variable, then add/subtract to eliminate.
  Worked Examples:
  - Solve x + 2y = 1, -x + y = 5 -> Add equations: 3y = 6 -> y = 2, x = -3.

- **Page**: 100
- **Status**: EXTRACTED
- **Extracted knowledge**:
  Concept: Absolute value algebraic balance.
  Rules:
  - For p >= 0, |f(x)| = p => f(x) = p or f(x) = -p.
  - If p < 0, |f(x)| = p has no solution.
  Worked Examples:
  - Solve |x+7| = 14 => x+7 = 14 => x = 7; or x+7 = -14 => x = -21.

  Rules:
  - Solving by factorization (e.g., x^2 + 6x + 9 = 0 => (x+3)^2 = 0 => x = -3).

### Page 103 -> Unit 2: Solving Equations
- **Source**: Grade 9 Maths Student Textbook 2Aug22.pdf
- **Textbook**: Grade 9 Mathematics Student Textbook
- **Unit/Section**: Unit 2: Solving Equations
- **Page**: 103
- **Status**: EXTRACTED
- **Extracted knowledge**:
  Concept: General solutions of quadratics.
  Theorems:
  - The Quadratic Formula: For ax^2 + bx + c = 0 (a != 0), the solutions are: x = (-b +- sqrt(b^2 - 4ac)) / (2a).
  Worked Examples:
  - Solve x^2 - 6x + 3 = 0 -> a=1, b=-6, c=3 -> x = (6 +- sqrt(36 - 12))/2 = 3 +- sqrt(6).

### Page 104 -> Unit 2: Solving Equations
- **Source**: Grade 9 Maths Student Textbook 2Aug22.pdf
- **Textbook**: Grade 9 Mathematics Student Textbook
- **Unit/Section**: Unit 2: Solving Equations
- **Page**: 104
- **Status**: EXTRACTED
- **Extracted knowledge**:
  Concept: Coefficients and root relationships.
  Rules:
  - Sum of roots: x1 + x2 = -b/a.
  - Product of roots: x1 * x2 = c/a.
  Worked Examples:
  - Find quadratic equation with root sum -6/5 and product 1/5 => 5x^2 + 6x + 1 = 0.

### Page 105 -> Unit 2: Solving Equations
- **Source**: Grade 9 Maths Student Textbook 2Aug22.pdf
- **Textbook**: Grade 9 Mathematics Student Textbook
- **Unit/Section**: Unit 2: Solving Equations
- **Page**: 105
- **Status**: EXTRACTED
- **Extracted knowledge**:
  Concept: Variable exponent calculations.
  Rules:
  - For a > 0, a != 1, a^x = a^y <=> x = y.
  Worked Examples:
  - Solve 9^(x+4) = 27 => 3^(2x+8) = 3^3 => 2x+8 = 3 => x = -5/2.

### Page 106 -> Unit 2: Solving Equations
- **Source**: Grade 9 Maths Student Textbook 2Aug22.pdf
- **Textbook**: Grade 9 Mathematics Student Textbook
- **Unit/Section**: Unit 2: Solving Equations
- **Page**: 106
- **Status**: EXTRACTED
- **Extracted knowledge**:
  Concept: Variable terms under radicals.
  Rules:
  1. Specify domain constraints (radicand >= 0).
  2. Isolate the radical.
  3. Square both sides.
  4. Solve and verify (discard extraneous roots).
  Worked Examples:
  - Solve sqrt(2x+3) + sqrt(x-2) = 4 => x = 3 (discarding x = 83 as extraneous).

- **Extracted knowledge**:
  Concept: PROJECTILE physics and work rate models.
  Formulas:
  - Work rate: 1/t_together = 1/t1 + 1/t2.
  - Projectile height: h = -16t^2 + v0*t + h0.
  Worked Examples:
  - Drop from 600m. Height: h = 600 - 16t^2. Find time to reach 200m.
    200 = 600 - 16t^2 => 16t^2 = 400 => t = 5 seconds.

  Concept: Absolute value boundaries.
  Rules:
  - |x| < p => -p < x < p.
  - |x| > p => x > p or x < -p.
  Worked Examples:
  - Solve |x+2| < 4 => -4 < x+2 < 4 => -6 < x < 2.

### Page 2 -> Unit 1: Relations and Functions
- **Source**: G10-Mathematics-STB-2023-web.pdf
- **Textbook**: Grade 10 Mathematics Student Textbook
- **Unit/Section**: Unit 1: Relations and Functions
- **Page**: 2
- **Status**: EXTRACTED
- **Extracted knowledge**:
  Concept: Domain, range, and coordinate mapping.
  Definitions:
  - Relation: A set of ordered pairs (x, y) mapping domain to range.
  - Function: A relation where each domain element is mapped to exactly one range element.
  - Graph of a function: The set of all coordinates (x, y) such that f(x) = y.
  - Linear Function: f(x) = ax + b.
  - Quadratic Function: f(x) = ax^2 + bx + c (a != 0) forming a parabola.
  Prerequisites:
  - G9 Coordinate geometry, linear equations.

### Page 68 -> Unit 2: Polynomial Functions
- **Source**: G10-Mathematics-STB-2023-web.pdf
- **Textbook**: Grade 10 Mathematics Student Textbook
- **Unit/Section**: Unit 2: Polynomial Functions
- **Page**: 68
- **Status**: EXTRACTED
- **Extracted knowledge**:
  Concept: Operations on polynomials.
  Theorems:
  - Remainder Theorem: If polynomial f(x) is divided by x - c, the remainder is f(c).
  - Factor Theorem: A polynomial f(x) has a factor x - c if and only if f(c) = 0.
  - Polynomial Division Theorem: f(x) = d(x)q(x) + r(x) where r(x) = 0 or deg(r) < deg(d).

### Page 21 -> Unit 2: Physical Quantities and Measurement
- **Source**: Physics  Grade 9 StudentTextbook Final version .pdf
- **Textbook**: Grade 9 Physics Student Textbook
- **Unit/Section**: Unit 2: Physical Quantities and Measurement
- **Page**: 21
- **Status**: EXTRACTED
- **Extracted knowledge**:
  Concept: Base and derived dimensions.
  Definitions:
  - Fundamental Quantities: Base quantities that don't depend on other dimensions.
  - Derived Quantities: Quantities depending on fundamental ones for measurement.
  Classifications:
  - Seven Basic Quantities: Length (m), Mass (kg), Time (s), Temperature (K), Current (A), Luminous Intensity (cd), Substance (mol).
  Formulas:
  - Speed = s/t
  - Density = m/V
  - Force = m * a
  - Work = F * displacement
  - Pressure = F / Area

  Concept: Modern biotechnology, bio-engineering, and bioinformatics.

  - Environment Management: Natural balance preservation.
  - Forensic Science: Biological coordinates applied to civil laws.

  - Activity 1.5: Sub-field importance.
  - Activity 1.6: Discussing major revolutionizing discoveries.
